「四野静得像在山上，满怀着无限的哀伤；方场那头，石板路上忽然响起一阵脚步的咔哒声，把那静寂衬托得愈发深沉。我立在窗前，凝望那株垂泪滴水的树，那下个不停的雨，那些一动不动的铅灰色云——一丝风也没有——仿佛听见了一切音乐中最悲伤的曲调，音声是苦楚与绝望，音符在哭喊、在啜泣。主题呈示出来，那主题本身就像被泪水打湿了似的。它以更尖利的哭声、更凄惶的哀求重复了一遍；它以一种苦涩的吐诉回响，泪落得更快，正如雨点从那垂泪的树上啪啪坠落。那音乐以悲伤的重叠、以不容宽赦的展开，在无情的推演中哀哭着，在我自己的心里滋长成一篇挽歌；它沉重，毫无希望；沉重得像那些凝定在天空里不动的铅灰色云。这悲哀的旋律没有舒解——反而来了一声更尖利的痛楚之音；随后，有那么一刻，仿佛为了给苦上加苦，响起了古怪而欢笑的一支曲调，一段欢快的笛音与奔涌的弦乐的乐句，和着舞步的欢乐回荡。可它被绝望的判词、被永世的劫数、被一句冷酷无情的判词碾成了齑粉；随着一阵突然的呼吸摇动了悬铃木湿漉漉的枝条、一阵急雨泼上路面，那内在音乐的最后几个音符，于我便成了一阵无望的恸哭。

===Original===

“I turned away from the window and looked at the dingy little room where we had laughed so well. It was a sad room enough, with its pale blue, stripy-patterned paper, its rickety old furniture and its feeble pictures. The only note of gaiety was on the dressing-table, where poor little Nelly had arranged some toys and trinkets and fantasies that she had bought for herself in the last few days. There was a silver-handled brush and a flagon of some scent that I liked, and a little brooch of olivines that had caught her fancy; and a powder-puff in a pretty gilt box. The sight of these foolish things cut me to the heart. But Nelly! She was standing by the bedside, half undressed, and she looked at me with the most piteous longing. I think that she had really grown fond of me. I suppose that I shall never forget the sad enchantment of her face, the flowing of her beautiful coppery hair about it; and the tears were wet on her cheeks. She half stretched out her bare arms to me and then let them fall. I had never known all her strange allurement before. I had refined and symbolised and made her into a sign of joy, and now before me she shone disarrayed﻿—not a symbol, but a woman, in the new intelligence that had come to me, and I longed for her. I had just enough strength and no more.”

===Chinese===

「我从窗前转过身，看了一眼我们笑得那样畅快的这间晦暗的小屋。那实在是一间伤心的屋子：淡蓝色的条纹壁纸，吱嘎作响的旧家具，几幅可怜巴巴的画。唯一的欢乐音符在梳妆台上，可怜的小内莉最近几天在那里摆了几件她给自己买的玩具、小饰物和好玩意儿：一把银柄的刷子，一瓶我喜欢闻的香水，一枚入了她的眼的橄榄石小胸针，还有一只装在漂亮金盒里的粉扑。这些傻气的小物件，看得我心如刀割。可是内莉！她站在床边，衣裳半解，带着最凄惶的渴念望着我。我想她是当真渐渐喜欢上我了。她那张脸上悲伤的魔力，她那头美丽的红铜色头发流泻其间，两颊泪痕犹湿——我想我永远不会忘记。她把两条赤裸的胳膊向我半伸，又垂了下去。我从前从来没有认识她全部奇异的魅力。我一直把她提炼过、象征过、把她变成欢乐的一个记号；而如今，在这份新来的觉悟之中，她衣衫半解地在我面前发光——不再是记号，而是一个女人——我渴望着她。我的气力刚刚够，也只刚刚够。」

## Epilogue / 尾声

===Original===
It is unfortunate﻿—or fortunate: that is a matter to be settled by the taste of the reader﻿—that with this episode of the visit to London all detailed material for the life of Ambrose Meyrick comes to an end. Odd scraps of information, stray notes and jottings are all that is available, and the rest of Meyrick’s life must be left in dim and somewhat legendary outline.

Personally, I think that this failure of documents is to be lamented. The four preceding chapters have, in the main, dealt with the years of boyhood, and therefore with a multitude of follies. One is inclined to wonder, as poor Nelly wondered, whether the lad was quite right in his head. It is possible that if we had fuller information as to his later years we might be able to dismiss him as decidedly eccentric, but well-meaning on the whole.

But, after all, I cannot be confident that he would get off so easily. Certainly he did not repeat the adventure of Little Russell Row, nor, so far as I am aware, did he address anyone besides his old schoolmaster in a Rabelaisian epistle. There are certain acts of lunacy which are like certain acts of heroism: they are hardly to be achieved twice by the same men.

===Chinese===
说来不幸——或者说有幸：这全凭读者的趣味去裁断——随着伦敦之行这桩插曲，安布罗斯·梅里克（Ambrose Meyrick）生平的全部详尽材料至此告终。所能依凭的，只剩零星见闻的残片、散佚的短札与随笔；梅里克其余的生平，只能留存在朦胧而略带传说色彩的轮廓之中。

就我个人而言，我以为文献的这一缺憾殊为可叹。前面四章主要写的是少年时代，因而写满了形形色色的蠢事。人们不禁要像可怜的内莉（Nelly）当年那样纳闷：这孩子的脑袋是否还清醒。倘若我们掌握他成年以后更充实的材料，或许就能把他一笔打发，断定他是个十足的怪人，但总的说来存心良善。

然而说到底，我可不敢担保他便能这样轻易过关。诚然，他没有重演小拉塞尔街（Little Russell Row）的那桩奇遇；据我所知，除了致他旧日的业师，他也不曾再以拉伯雷式的书信惊扰第二个人。但癫狂之举自有若干种，恰如英雄壮举自有若干种：同一个人极难把它们做上第二遍。

===Original===
But Meyrick continued to do odd things. He became a strolling player instead of becoming a scholar of Balliol. If he had proceeded to the University, he would have encountered the formative and salutary influence of Jowett. He wandered up and down the country for two or three years with the actors, and writes the following apostrophe to the memory of his old company.

“I take off my hat when I hear the old music, for I think of the old friends and the old days; of the theatre in the meadows by the sacred river, and the swelling song of the nightingales on sweet, spring nights. There is no doubt that we may safely hold with Plato his opinion, and safely may we believe that all brave earthly shows are but broken copies and dim lineaments of immortal things. Therefore, I hope and trust that I shall again be gathered unto the true Hathaway Company *quae sursum est*, which is the purged and exalted image of the lower, which plays forever a great mystery in the theatre of the meadows of asphodel, which wanders by the happy, shining streams, and drinks from an Eternal Cup in a high and blissful and everlasting Tavern. *Ave, cara sodalitas, ave semper.*”

Thus does he translate into wild speech crêpe hair and grease paints, dirty dressing-rooms and dirtier lodgings. And when his strolling days were over he settled down in London, paying occasional visits to his old home in the west. He wrote three or four books which are curious and interesting in their way, though they will never be popular. And finally he went on a strange errand to the East; and from the East there was for him no returning.

===Chinese===
但梅里克依旧尽干古怪的事情。他没有去当贝利奥尔学院（Balliol）的奖学金生，反倒做了一名流浪演员。他倘若进了大学，本可以领受乔伊特（Jowett）那塑造人、有益身心的熏陶。他跟着戏班在乡间上上下下漂泊了两三年，并写下如下这段呼语，追怀他旧日的班社。

「每当听见那旧日的乐声，我总要脱帽致敬，因为我想起旧日的朋友、旧日的岁月；想起圣河之畔草地上的剧场，想起甜美春夜里夜莺渐扬的歌声。毫无疑问，我们尽可以安然持守柏拉图（Plato）的见解，也尽可以安然相信：尘世一切壮美的演出，无非是永恒事物的残缺摹本与模糊轮廓。因此，我盼望并且笃信，我终将再度被聚拢进那真正的哈撒韦班社（Hathaway Company）——*quae sursum est*（那在上面的）——它是尘世下界那班社经涤净而升扬的影像，它永远在阿福花草地的剧场里搬演着一出大奥秘，沿着幸福而闪耀的溪流漫游，在一座崇高、极乐、永世长存的酒馆里，从一只永恒之杯中畅饮。*Ave, cara sodalitas, ave semper.*（万福，亲爱的社侣，永远万福。）」

就这样，他把皱纱假发和油彩粉墨、肮脏的化妆间和更肮脏的寓所，统统译成了这番癫狂的言语。流浪的日子结束后，他便在伦敦住了下来，偶尔回西部的老家看看。他写过三四本书，自有其奇趣与可观之处，却永远不会风行。末了他肩负一桩怪异的使命去了东方；而从东方，他没有再回来。

===Original===
It will be remembered that he speaks of a Celtic cup, which had been preserved in one family for many hundred years. On the death of the last “Keeper” this cup was placed in Meyrick’s charge. He received it with the condition that it was to be taken to a certain concealed shrine in Asia and there deposited in hands that would know how to hide its glories forever from the evil world.

He went on this journey into unknown regions, travelling by ragged roads and mountain passes, by the sandy wilderness and the mighty river. And he forded his way by the quaking and dubious track that winds in and out among the dangers and desolations of the Kevir﻿—the great salt slough.

He came at last to the place appointed and gave the word and the treasure to those who know how to wear a mask and to keep well the things which are committed to them, and then set out on his journey back. He had reached a point not very far from the gates of West and halted for a day or two amongst Christians, being tired out with a weary pilgrimage. But the Turks or the Kurds﻿—it does not matter which﻿—descended on the place and worked their customary works, and so Ambrose was taken by them.

===Chinese===
读者当会记得，他说起过一只凯尔特古杯，数百年来由一族人家世代秘藏。最后一位「守护人」（Keeper）故去之后，此杯便交托梅里克保管。他领受此杯时附有一项条件：须将它送往亚洲某处隐秘的圣龛，交到那懂得如何把它的荣光永远藏起、不叫邪恶世界窥见的人手中。

他踏上这趟深入未知之地的旅程，沿着崎岖的道路和山间隘口跋涉，穿越沙碛荒野与大河；末了又沿着那条颤摇而可疑的小径涉渡前行，小径在凯维尔（Kevir）——那浩大的盐沼——的重重危难与荒芜之间蜿蜒出没。

他终于抵达那指定的地方，把暗号连同宝物交给了那些懂得如何戴上面具、如何妥善守护所受托付之人，随即踏上归途。行至距西方之门已不甚远处，他在一群基督徒中间歇了一两日——长途跋涉，早已困乏不堪。但土耳其人或库尔德人——究竟是哪一伙并不要紧——扑向那地方，照例干起他们的老勾当，于是安布罗斯落入了他们手中。

===Original===
One of the native Christians, who had hidden himself from the miscreants, told afterwards how he saw “the stranger Ambrosian” brought out, and how they held before him the image of the Crucified that he might spit upon it and trample it under his feet. But he kissed the icon with great joy and penitence and devotion. So they bore him to a tree outside the village and crucified him there.

And after he had hung on the tree some hours, the infidels, enraged, as it is said, by the shining rapture of his face, killed him with their spears.

It was in this manner that Ambrose Meyrick gained Red Martyrdom and achieved the most glorious Quest and Adventure of the Sangraal.

===Chinese===
一名躲过了那帮匪徒的本地基督徒事后讲述：他如何亲眼看见「那位外乡人安布罗修」被押解出来；那些人又如何把受钉者的圣像捧到他面前，要他唾弃它、用脚把它践踏。但他怀着极大的喜乐、忏悔与虔诚，亲吻了圣像。于是他们把他抬到村外一棵树下，把他钉在了树上。

他在树上悬挂数个时辰之后，那些不信者——据说是被他脸上闪耀的极乐之光所激怒——用长矛把他杀害了。

安布罗斯·梅里克便这样获得了红色殉道（Red Martyrdom），成就了圣格拉尔寻求（the Quest of the Sangraal）之中最光荣的寻求与历险。

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. A highly Rabelaisian phrase is omitted. ↩︎
2. Translated from the Welsh verses quoted in the notebook. ↩︎

===Chinese===
1. 此处略去一个极具拉伯雷风味的词句。 ↩
2. 译自笔记本中所引的威尔士诗句。 ↩

===Original===
3. The following translation of these verses appeared in *Poems from the Old Bards*, by Taliesin, Bristol, 1812:
 
 
 “In Soar’s sweet valley, where the sound
 

 Of holy anthems once was heard
 

 From many a saint, the hills prolong
 

 Only the music of the bird.
 
 
 In Soar’s sweet valley, where the brook
 

 With many a ripple flows along,
 

 Delicious prospects meet the eye,
 

 The ear is charmed with *Phil’mel’s* song.
 
 
 In Soar’s sweet valley once a Maid,
 

 Despising worldly prospects gay,
 

 Resigned her note in earthly choirs
 

 Which now in Heaven must sound alway.

===Chinese===
3. 下列诗节的译文曾刊载于塔列辛（Taliesin）所著《老巴德诗钞》（*Poems from the Old Bards*），布里斯托尔，1812 年：
 
 
 「在索尔甜美的山谷，昔日诸圣
 

 曾唱响的圣颂之声已杳，
 

 而今群山所绵延不绝者，
 

 唯有鸟儿的乐音而已。
 
 
 在索尔甜美的山谷，清溪
 

 泛着千重涟漪潺潺流淌，
 

 悦目的胜景纷至沓来，
 

 耳畔迷醉于菲尔梅尔（*Phil’mel*，夜莺）之歌。
 
 
 在索尔甜美的山谷，曾有一位少女，
 

 鄙弃尘世浮华的前程，
 

 把歌喉留给了人间的唱诗班，
 

 而那歌声如今必在天上永世回响。

===Original===
 
 
 In Soar’s sweet valley David preached;
 

 His Gospel accents so beguiled
 

 The savage Britons, that they turned
 

 Their fiercest cries to music mild.
 
 
 In Soar’s sweet valley Cybi taught
 

 To haughty Prince the Holy Law,
 

 The way to Heaven he showed, and then
 

 The subject tribes inspired with awe.
 
 
 In Soar’s sweet valley still the song
 

 Of Phil’mel sounds and checks alarms.
 

 But when shall I once more renew
 

 Those heavenly hours in Gladys’ arms?”
 
 

===Chinese===
 
 
 在索尔甜美的山谷，大卫传道；
 

 福音的音调如此感化了
 

 野性的不列颠人，
 

 使他们最凶厉的呼号化作柔和的音乐。
 
 
 在索尔甜美的山谷，基比（Cybi）向
 

 傲慢的王侯传授神圣律法，
 

 指明天国之路，于是
 

 归附的部族心生敬畏。
 
 
 在索尔甜美的山谷，菲尔梅尔
 

 的歌声至今回荡，抚平惊惶。
 

 可我何时才能重温
 

 在格拉迪丝（Gladys）臂弯里的天国时光？”
 
 

===Original===
 “Taliesin” was the pseudonym of an amiable clergyman, the Reverend Owen Thomas, for many years curate of Llantrisant. He died in 1820, at the great age of eighty-four. His original poetry in Welsh was reputed as far superior to his translations, and he made a very valuable and curious collection of “Cymric Antiquities,” which remains in manuscript in the keeping of his descendants. ↩︎
4. A diamond. ↩︎

===Chinese===
「塔列辛」是一位和蔼可亲的教士的笔名——欧文·托马斯牧师（Owen Thomas），多年担任兰特里桑特（Llantrisant）的助理牧师。他卒于 1820 年，享年八十四岁高龄。人们公认，他用威尔士语创作的原诗远胜于他的译作；他还编纂了一部十分珍贵而奇特的《基姆里古物集》（“Cymric Antiquities”），至今仍以手稿形式保存在他的后人手中。 ↩
4. 一颗钻石。 ↩
